    /// Non-blocking: if the next RX descriptor is ours, return (index, frame length without FCS).
    /// The caller must hand the descriptor back with `rx_release(index)`.
    pub fn rx_poll(&mut self) -> Option<(usize, usize)> {
        let r = self.regs;
        let i = self.rx_next;
        unsafe {
            let o = read_volatile(addr_of!(RX_DESC.0[i].opts1));
            if o & r.desc_own != 0 {
                return None;
            }
            fence(Ordering::SeqCst);
            self.rx_next = (i + 1) % NRX;
            if o & (r.rx_err | r.first_frag | r.last_frag) != (r.first_frag | r.last_frag) {
                rx_release(r, i); // errored or multi-descriptor frame: drop it
                return None;
            }
            Some((i, ((o & r.rx_len_mask) as usize).saturating_sub(4).min(BUFSZ)))
        }
    }
}

/// Only valid between `rx_poll` returning (i, len) and `rx_release(i)` (net::Rx enforces this).
pub(crate) fn rx_buf(i: usize, len: usize) -> &'static [u8] {
        Ok(mut n) => {
            if cmdline.contains("nic=no8105e") {
                n.skip_8105e(); // A/B switch for the rtl_hw_start_8105e tweaks
            }
            kprintln!("nic: {} at {} {} mac={}", n.name(), n.bdf, n.bus, dhcp::Mac(n.mac));
            match n.init() {
                Ok(()) if cfg!(feature = "attended") => {
                    kprintln!("udplog: broadcasting this log to UDP 5514");
                    udplog::set_nic(&n);
                    udplog::dump(1);
                    udplog::dhcp_probe(&mut n);
                    udplog::dump(2);
                    if cmdline.contains("wdt=probe") {
                        wdt::pet();
                        kprintln!("wdt: HANG now (probe): cli; hlt, nobody pets; expect a reset ~60 s from here");
                        udplog::dump(3);
                        loop {
                            unsafe { asm!("cli; hlt") };
                        }

    pub fn poll(
        &mut self,
        timestamp: Instant,
        device: &mut (impl Device + ?Sized),
        sockets: &mut SocketSet<'_>,
    ) -> PollResult {
        self.inner.now = timestamp;

        let mut res = PollResult::None;

        self.poll_maintenance(timestamp);

        // Process ingress while there's packets available.
        loop {
            match self.socket_ingress(device, sockets) {
                PollIngressSingleResult::None => break,
                PollIngressSingleResult::PacketProcessed => {}
                PollIngressSingleResult::SocketStateChanged => res = PollResult::SocketStateChanged,
            }
        }

        // Process egress.
        loop {
            match self.poll_egress(timestamp, device, sockets) {
                PollResult::None => break,
                PollResult::SocketStateChanged => res = PollResult::SocketStateChanged,
            }
        }

        res
    }

    /// Transmit packets queued in the sockets.
    ///
    /// This function returns a value indicating whether the state of any socket
    /// might have changed.
    fn socket_ingress(
        &mut self,
        device: &mut (impl Device + ?Sized),
        sockets: &mut SocketSet<'_>,
    ) -> PollIngressSingleResult {
        let Some((rx_token, tx_token)) = device.receive(self.inner.now) else {
            return PollIngressSingleResult::None;
        };

        let rx_meta = rx_token.meta();
        rx_token.consume(|frame| {
            if frame.is_empty() {
                return PollIngressSingleResult::PacketProcessed;
            }

            match self.inner.caps.medium {
                #[cfg(feature = "medium-ethernet")]
                Medium::Ethernet => {
                    if let Some(packet) =
                        self.inner
                            .process_ethernet(sockets, rx_meta, frame, &mut self.fragments)
                        && let Err(err) =
                            self.inner.dispatch(tx_token, packet, &mut self.fragmenter)

**No—the nonce-reuse MUST-FIX remains open.** HEAD improves normal operation, but unauthenticated discovery can still force reuse. No files edited; source review plus a mocked-network execution of the actual Python client. I did not rerun Q35.

- **MUST-FIX — replayed discovery forces C2K nonce reuse.** [bmctl.py:40](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/bmctl.py:40) accepts an unauthenticated `(boot_id, session)` and starts sequence 1. A LAN attacker can race the real response with an old `who` response, causing separate invocations/clients to encrypt different commands with the same key and nonce—even across actual kernel reboots. Server rejection or session eviction cannot undo ciphertext exposure. I reproduced this with `ping` and `reboot`: ciphertext XOR matched plaintext XOR. This violates [RFC 8439’s nonce requirement](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8439.html#section-4). **Fix:** include a fresh client-generated random challenge in discovery, authenticate the response and complete transcript, and bind that challenge into traffic-key derivation before sending commands. Authenticating an otherwise replayable response alone is insufficient.

- **SHOULD-FIX — unauthenticated session eviction.** [bmctl.rs:201](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:201): four permitted `who` requests evict every existing session, including active transfers and cached results. The full-ID comparison correctly prevents modulo-slot aliasing; eviction itself does **not** recreate keys. Allocate/evict authenticated sessions only after proof of key possession, and protect active sessions.

- **SHOULD-FIX — session-ID wrap.** `next_sess += 1` wraps in release, eventually reissuing IDs and keys within the same boot. At ten allocations/second this takes approximately **13.6 years**, requiring authenticated activity to prevent idle reset: remote practicality is negligible, but “never repeats” is false. Use checked increment and refuse allocation/rekey before exhaustion. Python’s `u64` packing fails rather than wrapping its sequence; the kernel rejects sequences below the accepted maximum.

- **SHOULD-FIX — RX budget has two gaps.** [net.rs:48](~/nc110-baremetal/src/net.rs:48) bounds **receive attempts**, including empty/error attempts; this successfully bounds steady-state ingress to at most 32 frames per poll. However, DHCP polling has no budget reset. Also, the supposedly unlimited default `u32::MAX` is decremented even for SSH, eventually disabling its reception permanently. Make unlimited explicit and budget DHCP too.

- **SHOULD-FIX — cached replies introduce replay amplification.** [bmctl.rs:223](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:223): a sniffer can repeatedly replay the latest authenticated `log` request with a spoofed source and obtain large cached responses without knowing the key. Bind sessions to endpoints and rate-limit retransmissions. Byte-identical cache resend is cryptographically safe and does not refresh the idle timer.

**NIT / remaining checks:** The fixed-length SHA-256 derivation has no evident concatenation ambiguity or usable length-extension attack here; HKDF is preferable, but this hash choice is not the blocker. Cross-boot separation still depends on boot-ID non-repetition; the Atom jitter source remains unvalidated. Direction separation, log-offset clamping, and cached retry encryption look correct. Q35 still merely prints `stats`, and its other-boot test changes AAD rather than exercising reboot/key renewal; ten passing checks do not cover the discovery attack.

**Not yet a sound security base for peek or kernel loading.** After fixing session establishment and lifecycle, it is a reasonable foundation. RAM peek exposes resident secrets; NIC MMIO reads can have side effects. A loader additionally needs checked staging bounds, authenticated image completeness, and an explicit commit step before execution.

**5/10.**

     1	//! bmctl: authenticated UDP control port (5515) so an agent can inspect and drive the kernel over the LAN, the way
     2	//! the DS client is driven over net2. Frame: [seq u64 LE][ciphertext][16-byte tag], ChaCha20-Poly1305 (RFC 8439),
     3	//! nonce = b"C2K\0"|b"K2C\0" ++ seq, AAD = this boot's random 16-byte id. Master = SHA-256("nc110-bmctl-v1" ++
     4	//! client seed from the key module): nothing secret is compiled in. Handshake (Codex MUST-FIX x2, nonce reuse):
     5	//! `who` ++ a fresh random 16-byte client nonce Nc is the only clear-text request; the reply carries the boot id, a
     6	//! NEW session number and check = SHA-256("nc110-bmctl-v1 who" ++ master ++ Nc ++ boot id ++ session)[..16].
     7	//! Traffic key = SHA-256("nc110-bmctl-v1 session" ++ master ++ boot id ++ session ++ Nc); seq counts from 1. A
     8	//! replayed or forged `who` reply cannot make two client runs share a key, because Nc is fresh per run, and the
     9	//! kernel never reissues a session number in a boot. A session binds to the endpoint of its first good frame.
    10	//! A duplicate of the last accepted seq gets the cached reply bytes back (safe UDP retries, nothing re-executed).
    11	//! The loop pets the watchdog; 10 minutes without a valid command -> 0xCF9 reset.
    12	use crate::net::Nic;
    13	use crate::nic::Rtl;
    14	use crate::{console, kprintln, rng, time, wdt};
    15	use chacha20::cipher::{KeyIvInit, StreamCipher};
    16	use chacha20::ChaCha20;
    17	use poly1305::universal_hash::{KeyInit, UniversalHash};
    18	use poly1305::Poly1305;
    19	use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
    20	use smoltcp::iface::{Config, Interface, SocketSet, SocketStorage};
    21	use smoltcp::socket::{dhcpv4, udp};
    22	use smoltcp::time::Instant;
    23	use smoltcp::wire::{EthernetAddress, HardwareAddress, IpAddress, IpCidr, IpEndpoint, Ipv4Address};
    24	
    25	const PORT: u16 = 5515;
    26	const IDLE_MS: i64 = 600_000;
    27	
    28	static mut SESS: [Option<Sess>; 16] = [const { None }; 16];
    29	static mut SOCKS: [SocketStorage<'static>; 2] = [SocketStorage::EMPTY; 2];
    30	static mut RX_META: [udp::PacketMetadata; 8] = [udp::PacketMetadata::EMPTY; 8];
    31	static mut RX_BUF: [u8; 8192] = [0; 8192];
    32	static mut TX_META: [udp::PacketMetadata; 8] = [udp::PacketMetadata::EMPTY; 8];
    33	static mut TX_BUF: [u8; 8192] = [0; 8192];

    35	fn now() -> Instant {
    36	    Instant::from_millis(time::now_ms())
    37	}
    38	
    39	fn nonce(dir: &[u8; 4], seq: u64) -> [u8; 12] {
    40	    let mut n = [0u8; 12];
    41	    n[..4].copy_from_slice(dir);
    42	    n[4..].copy_from_slice(&seq.to_le_bytes());
    43	    n
    44	}
    45	
    46	/// RFC 8439 tag over aad and ct with the one-time key from keystream block 0.
    47	fn tag(pk: &[u8; 32], aad: &[u8], ct: &[u8]) -> [u8; 16] {
    48	    let mut m = Poly1305::new(pk.into());
    49	    m.update_padded(aad);
    50	    m.update_padded(ct);
    51	    let mut lens = [0u8; 16];
    52	    lens[..8].copy_from_slice(&(aad.len() as u64).to_le_bytes());
    53	    lens[8..].copy_from_slice(&(ct.len() as u64).to_le_bytes());
    54	    m.update_padded(&lens);
    55	    m.finalize().into()
    56	}
    57	
    58	fn cipher(key: &[u8; 32], n: &[u8; 12]) -> (ChaCha20, [u8; 32]) {
    59	    let mut c = ChaCha20::new(key.into(), n.into());
    60	    let mut b0 = [0u8; 64];
    61	    c.apply_keystream(&mut b0); // block 0 -> Poly1305 key; the stream continues at block 1
    62	    let mut pk = [0u8; 32];
    63	    pk.copy_from_slice(&b0[..32]);
    64	    (c, pk)
    65	}
    66	
    67	pub fn seal(key: &[u8; 32], n: &[u8; 12], aad: &[u8], buf: &mut [u8]) -> [u8; 16] {
    68	    let (mut c, pk) = cipher(key, n);
    69	    c.apply_keystream(buf);
    70	    tag(&pk, aad, buf)
    71	}
    72	
    73	pub fn open(key: &[u8; 32], n: &[u8; 12], aad: &[u8], buf: &mut [u8], t: &[u8]) -> bool {
    74	    let (mut c, pk) = cipher(key, n);
    75	    let want = tag(&pk, aad, buf);
    76	    if t.len() != 16 {
    77	        return false;
    78	    }
    79	    let diff = want.iter().zip(t).fold(0u8, |a, (x, y)| a | (x ^ y));
    80	    if diff != 0 {
    81	        return false;
    82	    }
    83	    c.apply_keystream(buf);
    84	    true
    85	}
    86	
    87	pub fn session_key(master: &[u8; 32], boot_id: &[u8; 16], sess: u32, nc: &[u8; 16]) -> [u8; 32] {
    88	    let mut h = Sha256::new();
    89	    h.update(b"nc110-bmctl-v1 session");
    90	    h.update(master);
    91	    h.update(boot_id);
    92	    h.update(sess.to_le_bytes());
    93	    h.update(nc);
    94	    h.finalize().into()
    95	}
    96	
    97	fn who_check(master: &[u8; 32], nc: &[u8; 16], boot_id: &[u8; 16], sess: u32) -> [u8; 32] {
    98	    let mut h = Sha256::new();
    99	    h.update(b"nc110-bmctl-v1 who");
   100	    h.update(master);
   101	    h.update(nc);
   102	    h.update(boot_id);
   103	    h.update(sess.to_le_bytes());
   104	    h.finalize().into()
   105	}

   115	    reply_len: usize,
   116	}

   118	pub fn derive_key(seed: &[u8; 32]) -> [u8; 32] {
   119	    let mut h = Sha256::new();
   120	    h.update(b"nc110-bmctl-v1");
   121	    h.update(seed);
   122	    h.finalize().into()
   123	}

   125	fn hex(b: &[u8], out: &mut heapless::String<64>) {
   126	    for x in b {
   127	        let _ = core::fmt::write(out, format_args!("{:02x}", x));
   128	    }
   129	}

   131	pub fn run(nic: Rtl, seed: [u8; 32]) {
   132	    if rng::init(false).is_err() {
   133	        return;
   134	    }
   135	    let key = derive_key(&seed);
   136	    let mut boot_id = [0u8; 16];
   137	    let mut rs = [0u8; 8];
   138	    if rng::fill(&mut boot_id).is_err() || rng::fill(&mut rs).is_err() {
   139	        kprintln!("bmctl: entropy failed -> not starting");
   140	        return;
   141	    }
   142	    let mut bid = heapless::String::<64>::new();
   143	    hex(&boot_id, &mut bid);
   144	    let mac = nic.mac;
   145	    let mut dev = Nic(nic);
   146	    let mut cfg = Config::new(HardwareAddress::Ethernet(EthernetAddress(mac)));
   147	    cfg.random_seed = u64::from_le_bytes(rs);
   148	    let mut iface = Interface::new(cfg, &mut dev, now());
   149	    let mut sockets = SocketSet::new(unsafe { &mut *core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(SOCKS) }[..].as_mut());
   150	    let dhcp_h = sockets.add(dhcpv4::Socket::new());
   151	    let udp_h = sockets.add(udp::Socket::new(
   152	        udp::PacketBuffer::new(unsafe { &mut *core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(RX_META) }[..].as_mut(), unsafe { &mut *core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(RX_BUF) }[..].as_mut()),
   153	        udp::PacketBuffer::new(unsafe { &mut *core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(TX_META) }[..].as_mut(), unsafe { &mut *core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(TX_BUF) }[..].as_mut()),
   154	    ));
   155	    let t0 = time::now_ms();
   156	    let mut ip = None;
   157	    while ip.is_none() && time::now_ms() - t0 < 15_000 {
   158	        wdt::pet();
   159	        crate::net::rx_budget(Some(32));
   160	        iface.poll(now(), &mut dev, &mut sockets);
   161	        if let Some(dhcpv4::Event::Configured(c)) = sockets.get_mut::<dhcpv4::Socket>(dhcp_h).poll() {
   162	            iface.update_ip_addrs(|a| {
   163	                a.clear();
   164	                let _ = a.push(IpCidr::Ipv4(c.address));
   165	            });
   166	            if let Some(gw) = c.router {
   167	                let _ = iface.routes_mut().add_default_ipv4_route(gw);
   168	            }
   169	            ip = Some(c.address);
   170	        }
   171	    }
   172	    let Some(ip) = ip else {
   173	        kprintln!("bmctl: no DHCP lease in 15 s -> not starting");
   174	        return;
   175	    };
   176	    sockets.get_mut::<udp::Socket>(udp_h).bind(PORT).expect("bind");
   177	    kprintln!("bmctl: listening on {}:{} boot id {} (idle reset after {} s)", ip.address(), PORT, bid, IDLE_MS / 1000);
   178	    crate::udplog::dump(5);
   179	
   180	    let (mut last_ok, mut last_hello) = (time::now_ms(), 0i64);
   181	    let (mut ok, mut bad, mut replay, mut retx) = (0u32, 0u32, 0u32, 0u32);
   182	    let (mut next_sess, mut who_window, mut who_count) = (1u32, 0i64, 0u32);
   183	    let sess = unsafe { &mut *core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(SESS) };
   184	    let mut reboot_at: Option<i64> = None;
   185	    loop {
   186	        wdt::pet();
   187	        crate::net::rx_budget(Some(32)); // bounded ingress per poll so the deadline checks below always run
   188	        iface.poll(now(), &mut dev, &mut sockets);
   189	        let t = time::now_ms();
   190	        if t - last_ok > IDLE_MS {
   191	            kprintln!("bmctl: idle {} s -> reset", IDLE_MS / 1000);
   192	            return;
   193	        }
   194	        if reboot_at.is_some_and(|r| t > r) {
   195	            kprintln!("bmctl: reboot requested");
   196	            wdt::hard_reset();
   197	        }
   198	        let sock = sockets.get_mut::<udp::Socket>(udp_h);
   199	        if t - last_hello > 2000 {
   200	            last_hello = t;
   201	            let mut h = heapless::String::<128>::new();
   202	            let _ = core::fmt::write(&mut h, format_args!("nc110 hello bmctl {}:{} boot {}", ip.address(), PORT, bid));
   203	            let _ = sock.send_slice(h.as_bytes(), IpEndpoint::new(IpAddress::Ipv4(Ipv4Address::BROADCAST), 5514));
   204	        }
   205	        let mut buf = [0u8; 1500];
   206	        let Ok((n, meta)) = sock.recv_slice(&mut buf) else { continue };
   207	        let from = meta.endpoint;
   208	        if n == 3 + 16 && &buf[..3] == b"who" {
   209	            if t - who_window > 1000 {
   210	                (who_window, who_count) = (t, 0);
   211	            }
   212	            who_count += 1;
   213	            if who_count > 10 {
   214	                continue; // global cap: at most 10 clear-text replies per second (reflection)
   215	            }
   216	            // Slot: empty, else a never-authenticated one, else the least recently used authenticated one. Only key
   217	            // holders create authenticated sessions, so an unauthenticated `who` flood can only churn pending ones.
   218	            let slot = (0..sess.len()).find(|&i| sess[i].is_none())
   219	                .or_else(|| (0..sess.len()).find(|&i| sess[i].as_ref().is_some_and(|x| x.peer.is_none())))
   220	                .or_else(|| (0..sess.len()).min_by_key(|&i| sess[i].as_ref().map_or(i64::MIN, |x| x.last_t)));
   221	            let (Some(slot), Some(next)) = (slot, next_sess.checked_add(1)) else { continue };

   223	            next_sess = next;
   224	            let mut nc = [0u8; 16];
   225	            nc.copy_from_slice(&buf[3..19]);
   226	            sess[slot] = Some(Sess { id, key: session_key(&key, &boot_id, id, &nc), last_seq: 0, peer: None, last_t: t, retx: 0, reply: [0; 8 + 1300 + 16], reply_len: 0 });
   227	            let mut chk = heapless::String::<64>::new();
   228	            hex(&who_check(&key, &nc, &boot_id, id)[..16], &mut chk);
   229	            let mut h = heapless::String::<160>::new();
   230	            let _ = core::fmt::write(&mut h, format_args!("nc110 boot {} session {} check {}", bid, id, chk));
   231	            let _ = sock.send_slice(h.as_bytes(), from);
   232	            continue;
   233	        }
   234	        // Frame: [session u32 LE][seq u64 LE][ct][tag]
   235	        if n < 4 + 8 + 16 {
   236	            bad += 1;
   237	            continue;
   238	        }
   239	        let sid = u32::from_le_bytes(buf[..4].try_into().unwrap());
   240	        let Some(se) = sess.iter_mut().flatten().find(|x| x.id == sid && x.peer.is_none_or(|p| p == from)) else {
   241	            bad += 1;
   242	            continue;
   243	        };
   244	        let seq = u64::from_le_bytes(buf[4..12].try_into().unwrap());
   245	        let (body, tg) = buf[12..n].split_at_mut(n - 12 - 16);
   246	        if !open(&se.key, &nonce(b"C2K\0", seq), &boot_id, body, tg) {
   247	            bad += 1;
   248	            continue;
   249	        }
   250	        if seq == se.last_seq && se.reply_len > 0 {
   251	            if se.retx >= 5 {
   252	                continue; // cap resends of one reply
   253	            }
   254	            se.retx += 1;
   255	            retx += 1; // retry of the last request: same bytes again (to the bound peer), nothing re-executed
   256	            let _ = sock.send_slice(&se.reply[..se.reply_len], from);
   257	            continue;
   258	        }
   259	        if seq <= se.last_seq {
   260	            replay += 1;
   261	            continue;
   262	        }
   263	        se.last_seq = seq;
   264	        se.peer = Some(from);
   265	        se.last_t = t;
   266	        se.retx = 0;
   267	        last_ok = t;
   268	        ok += 1;
   269	        let mut out = [0u8; 1300];
   270	        let len = {
   271	            let mut w = Writer { b: &mut out, n: 0 };
   272	            let cmd = core::str::from_utf8(body).unwrap_or("");
   273	            let mut it = cmd.split(' ');
   274	            match it.next().unwrap_or("") {
   275	                "ping" => {
   276	                    let _ = core::fmt::write(&mut w, format_args!("pong uptime_ms={} ip={}", t, ip.address()));
   277	                }
   278	                "stats" => {
   279	                    let _ = core::fmt::write(&mut w, format_args!("ok={} bad={} replay={} retx={} sessions_issued={} log_bytes={}", ok, bad, replay, retx, next_sess - 1, console::captured().len()));
   280	                }
   281	                "log" => {
   282	                    let off: usize = it.next().and_then(|x| x.parse().ok()).unwrap_or(0);
   283	                    let l = console::captured();
   284	                    let st = off.min(l.len());
   285	                    let s = &l[st..st.saturating_add(1200).min(l.len())];
   286	                    w.put(s);
   287	                }
   288	                "reboot" => {
   289	                    let _ = core::fmt::write(&mut w, format_args!("rebooting in 500 ms"));
   290	                    reboot_at = Some(t + 500);
   291	                }
   292	                _ => {
   293	                    let _ = core::fmt::write(&mut w, format_args!("err unknown command (ping|stats|log <off>|reboot)"));
   294	                }
   295	            }
   296	            w.n
   297	        };
   298	        let pkt = &mut se.reply;
   299	        pkt[..8].copy_from_slice(&seq.to_le_bytes());
   300	        pkt[8..8 + len].copy_from_slice(&out[..len]);
   301	        let tg = seal(&se.key, &nonce(b"K2C\0", seq), &boot_id, &mut pkt[8..8 + len]);
   302	        pkt[8 + len..8 + len + 16].copy_from_slice(&tg);
   303	        se.reply_len = 8 + len + 16;
   304	        let _ = sock.send_slice(&se.reply[..se.reply_len], from);
   305	    }
   306	}
   307	
   308	struct Writer<'a> {
   309	    b: &'a mut [u8],
   310	    n: usize,
   311	}
   312	impl Writer<'_> {
   313	    fn put(&mut self, s: &[u8]) {
   314	        let k = s.len().min(self.b.len() - self.n);
   315	        self.b[self.n..self.n + k].copy_from_slice(&s[..k]);
   316	        self.n += k;
   317	    }
   318	}
   319	impl core::fmt::Write for Writer<'_> {
   320	    fn write_str(&mut self, s: &str) -> core::fmt::Result {
   321	        self.put(s.as_bytes());
   322	        Ok(())
   323	    }
   324	}
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""bmctl client for the NC110 kernel's authenticated UDP control port (see src/bmctl.rs).
     3	
     4	Usage: tools/.venv/bin/python tools/bmctl.py [--host 192.168.50.120] [--key test-keys/client.key] <command...>
     5	  commands: ping | stats | log [offset] | logall | reboot
     6	The key file is the same 72-byte multiboot key module the kernel gets (magic 8 + ed25519 seed 32 + pub 32);
     7	master = SHA-256("nc110-bmctl-v1" + seed). Handshake: send "who" + fresh random 16-byte Nc; the reply carries boot id,
     8	session and check = SHA-256("nc110-bmctl-v1 who" + master + Nc + boot + session)[:16], verified here; traffic key =
     9	SHA-256("nc110-bmctl-v1 session" + master + boot + session_u32_le + Nc); seq counts from 1 per session. Uses the `cryptography` package's ChaCha20Poly1305,
    10	an implementation independent of the kernel's, so agreement is a real interop check.
    11	"""
    12	import argparse, hashlib, hmac, os, socket, struct, sys
    13	from cryptography.hazmat.primitives.ciphers.aead import ChaCha20Poly1305
    14	
    15	ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    16	ap.add_argument("--host", default="192.168.50.120")
    17	ap.add_argument("--port", type=int, default=5515)
    18	ap.add_argument("--key", default="test-keys/client.key")
    19	ap.add_argument("cmd", nargs="+")
    20	a = ap.parse_args()
    21	km = open(a.key, "rb").read()
    22	assert len(km) == 72, "key module must be 72 bytes"
    23	master = hashlib.sha256(b"nc110-bmctl-v1" + km[8:40]).digest()
    24	s = socket.socket(socket.AF_INET, socket.SOCK_DGRAM)
    25	s.settimeout(2.0)
    26	
    27	
    28	def rpc(raw):
    29	    for _ in range(3):  # a retry of the same frame gets the kernel's cached reply, nothing runs twice
    30	        s.sendto(raw, (a.host, a.port))
    31	        try:
    32	            while True:
    33	                d, src = s.recvfrom(2048)
    34	                if src[0] == socket.gethostbyname(a.host):
    35	                    return d
    36	        except socket.timeout:
    37	            continue
    38	    sys.exit(f"no reply from {a.host}:{a.port}")
    39	
    40	
    41	nc = os.urandom(16)
    42	who = rpc(b"who" + nc).decode().split()
    43	if len(who) != 7 or who[:2] != ["nc110", "boot"] or who[3] != "session" or who[5] != "check" or len(who[2]) != 32:
    44	    sys.exit(f"bad who reply: {who}")
    45	boot, sid = bytes.fromhex(who[2]), int(who[4])
    46	want = hashlib.sha256(b"nc110-bmctl-v1 who" + master + nc + boot + struct.pack("<I", sid)).digest()[:16]
    47	if not hmac.compare_digest(want, bytes.fromhex(who[6])):
    48	    sys.exit("who reply failed its check (wrong key, or a forged/replayed reply)")
    49	aead = ChaCha20Poly1305(hashlib.sha256(b"nc110-bmctl-v1 session" + master + boot + struct.pack("<I", sid) + nc).digest())
    50	SEQ = [0]
    51	
    52	
    53	def call(cmd):
    54	    SEQ[0] += 1  # per-session counter from 1; a fresh session per run, so (key, nonce) never repeats
    55	    seq = SEQ[0]
    56	    ct = aead.encrypt(b"C2K\0" + struct.pack("<Q", seq), cmd.encode(), boot)
    57	    r = rpc(struct.pack("<IQ", sid, seq) + ct)
    58	    rseq = struct.unpack("<Q", r[:8])[0]
    59	    if rseq != seq:
    60	        sys.exit(f"reply seq {rseq} != {seq}")
    61	    return aead.decrypt(b"K2C\0" + struct.pack("<Q", seq), r[8:], boot)
    62	
    63	
    64	cmd = " ".join(a.cmd)
    65	if cmd == "logall":
    66	    off, out = 0, b""
    67	    while True:
    68	        chunk = call(f"log {off}")
    69	        if not chunk:
    70	            break
    71	        out += chunk
    72	        off += len(chunk)
    73	    sys.stdout.write(out.decode(errors="replace"))
    74	else:
    75	    print(call(cmd).decode(errors="replace"))

2. **Unbounded network polling can starve software reset deadlines.** [bmctl.rs:143](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:143)

   The pinned smoltcp `Interface::poll()` drains ingress until empty and explicitly documents potentially unbounded work under sustained traffic. Both idle and requested-reboot checks occur afterward.

   An adequately fast LAN flood could therefore prevent reaching those checks. It also prevents further watchdog pets, so a functioning hardware watchdog should eventually fire; this is **not** an attack that keeps petting forever. The repository’s warning that TCO can hang this real board makes reliance on that fallback particularly concerning.

   Bound ingress work per iteration and check deadlines between batches. Actual exploitability at the netbook’s receive rate remains untested.

3. **UDP retries lose successful results, which matters for future mutations.** [bmctl.py:26](~/nc110-baremetal/tools/bmctl.py:26)

   If a command executes but its reply is lost, `rpc()` resends the identical request; the kernel rejects it as replay without returning the result. The caller reports failure despite successful execution.

   Before adding writes/loading, cache the exact encrypted response for an authenticated duplicate request and resend those bytes without executing again. Do not generate a different response under the original nonce.

4. **`who` is an unrestricted small reflector.** [bmctl.rs:164](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:164)

   Three request bytes produce 43 response bytes: roughly 14× UDP-payload amplification, substantially less including packet overhead. Add a global response-rate limit; source-only limits are weak against spoofing.

   `log` is different: it requires authentication. A sniffer can race a captured, not-yet-accepted request with a spoofed source and redirect its one encrypted response, but accepted requests cannot be replayed repeatedly for amplification. I would not classify `log` as an unauthenticated bulk reflector.

5. **An authenticated log offset can panic the kernel.** [bmctl.rs:202](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:202)

   `off + 1200` can overflow. For `usize::MAX`, release arithmetic wraps to 1199; with a sufficiently long log, the slice start exceeds its end and panics. The panic handler halts.

   Clamp the start first, then use saturating addition for the end. Low security severity today because the caller already has reboot authority, but worth fixing before expanding the parser.

**NIT / hardening**

- Use an established constant-time equality primitive and reject non-16-byte tags first. The current XOR/OR fold has no source-level mismatch early exit, but compiler-level constant-time behavior was not verified. **There is no truncated-tag acceptance bug:** length is checked before success, and the network caller always supplies exactly 16 bytes.
- Validate discovery response shape and boot-ID length, and filter reply endpoints. Today any sender can win `recvfrom()`, spoof discovery, or cause parsing/decryption failure. Endpoint filtering improves robustness but cannot authenticate against LAN source spoofing.
- Make the Q35 script fail its process on failed expectations. Currently several failures merely print `FAIL`; interop coverage also does not substitute for RFC vectors and boundary-length tests.

The remaining requested checks:

- **Construction:** consuming all 64 bytes of ChaCha block 0 correctly leaves encryption at counter 1; the first 32 bytes supply the Poly1305 key. Separate zero-padding of AAD/ciphertext and little-endian byte lengths are correct. Authentication precedes decryption.
- **Reflection:** `C2K\0` and `K2C\0` provide distinct nonces. Feeding a reply back as a command fails authentication.
- **Replay/petting:** only authenticated, increasing sequences advance `last_seq` and `last_ok`. `who`, junk, and accepted-frame replays cannot keep the idle timer alive. Captured but undelivered commands can be delayed and accepted once; sequence ordering provides no expiration.
- **Boot ID:** secrecy or unpredictability is unnecessary for its present AAD role; non-repetition matters. Prediction alone does not forge commands. Repetition permits old-boot replay after sequence reset. The RNG explicitly says its entropy claim has not been established on this Atom; health tests do not prove boot uniqueness.
- **SSH seed reuse:** the fixed domain prefix and high-entropy seed are reasonable separation for this narrow derivation; I see no direct cross-protocol break. Separate provisioning would improve compromise isolation. Arbitrary RAM reads or loaded code can expose the SSH seed and control keys.
- **Future authority:** unrestricted RAM writes, NIC DMA programming, and kernel loading amount to full kernel authority. “RAM + NIC MMIO only” does not meaningfully contain a compromised controller or loader.

Fix nonce/session design before extending authority, and make retries and reset deadlines dependable before relying on it remotely.

**Security readiness: 5/10.**

The fresh client nonce closes the previous replay-to-nonce-reuse path. I found a remaining eviction gap: once all 16 slots are authenticated, an unauthenticated `who` evicts the oldest session. The flood test leaves pending slots available, so it misses that case.

codex
**The nonce-reuse MUST-FIX is closed for the supplied client**, assuming normal cryptographic security and no client sequence rollback. Fresh `Nc` separates client runs; checked session allocation prevents within-boot reuse; direction prefixes separate requests/replies; retries resend existing ciphertext. “Impossible” remains a cryptographic assumption, not an absolute guarantee.

Reviewed HEAD `e28f276`; no edits or Q35 rerun.

- **MUST-FIX:** No new authentication or nonce-reuse break found.

- **SHOULD-FIX — authenticated sessions can still be evicted by `who`.** [bmctl.rs:218](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:218): when all 16 slots are authenticated, one unauthenticated request takes the LRU slot. Subsequent requests churn that pending slot, so this is limited eviction, not wholesale flushing. Normal client invocations eventually fill the table. Keep pending state separate or use cookies, and evict authenticated sessions only after authentication.

- **SHOULD-FIX — peer binding permits a first-frame race.** [bmctl.rs:240](~/nc110-baremetal/src/bmctl.rs:240): a sniffer racing the first valid command from another endpoint can bind the session there and deny the real client. This exposes no plaintext and enables no new command forgery. Bind the discovery endpoint into session state—and preferably the authenticated transcript. Source spoofing remains possible.

- **SHOULD-FIX — tests overstate coverage.** Case 8 tests rejection under a different key, not the actual client’s rejection of replayed discovery. Case 9 never fills all slots with authenticated sessions. Add those cases, endpoint-race coverage, and verification that the sixth duplicate gets no reply.

- **NIT / crypto hardening:** `who_check` binds the intended fixed-length fields. I see no usable length-extension or cross-domain attack from sharing the master under distinct prefixes; a separate MAC key is **not an urgent security fix**. Prefer a labelled derived key and standard [HMAC-SHA-256](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc2104) before extending the protocol.

The **five-resend cap is sound**: cached bytes only, no execution or idle-timer refresh. A spoofing sniffer can consume those five attempts, causing availability loss. Checked session exhaustion and both RX-budget fixes look correct.

**Before peek/loading:** fix authenticated-session eviction and define transfer recovery after lost replies/session loss. Peek needs checked address arithmetic, bounded lengths, explicit readable ranges, and a decision about exposing resident keys; MMIO reads need register-specific handling. Loading needs isolated bounded staging, authenticated image length/digest and completeness, checked destinations/entry point, and an explicit idempotent commit. Validate boot-ID non-repetition on the Atom: repeating a boot ID lets an attacker replay old `Nc` values and recreate old sessions despite honest clients generating fresh randomness.

**7/10.**
